Christopher Hitchens and Chatterjee (author of <i>The Final Verdict</i>, a book critical of Mother Teresa) spoke to the tribunal; according to Vatican officials, the allegations raised were investigated by the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Brown_149-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brown-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The group found no obstacle to Mother Teresa's canonisation, and issued its <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la"><a href="/wiki/Nihil_obstat" title="Nihil obstat">nihil obstat</a></i></span> on 21 April 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Because of the attacks on her, some Catholic writers called her a <a href="/wiki/Sign_of_contradiction" title="Sign of contradiction">sign of contradiction</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Mother Teresa was beatified on 19 October 2003, and was known by Catholics as "<a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Blessed</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization_2">Canonization</span></h3>
<p>On 17 December 2015, the Vatican Press Office confirmed that <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> recognised a second miracle attributed to Mother Teresa: the healing of a Brazilian man with multiple <a href="/wiki/Brain_tumours" class="mw-redirect" title="Brain tumours">brain tumours</a> back in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-miracle2_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-miracle2-159">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> The miracle first came to the attention of the postulation (officials managing the cause) during the events of <a href="/wiki/World_Youth_Day_2013" title="World Youth Day 2013">World Youth Day 2013</a> when the pope was in Brazil that July. A subsequent investigation took place in Brazil from 19–26 June 2015 which was later transferred to the Congregation for the Causes of Saints who issued a decree recognizing the investigation to be completed.<sup id="cite_ref-miracle2_159-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-miracle2-159">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Francis canonised her at a ceremony on 4 September 2016 in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a> in Vatican City. Tens of thousands of people witnessed the ceremony, including 15 government delegations and 1,500 homeless people from across Italy.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:1_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> It was televised live on the Vatican channel and streamed online; Skopje, Mother Teresa's hometown, announced a week-long celebration of her canonisation.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> In India, a special Mass was celebrated by the Missionaries of Charity in Calcutta.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Co-Patron_of_Calcutta_Archdiocese">Co-Patron of Calcutta Archdiocese</span></h2>
<p>On 4 September 2017, during a celebration honouring the 1st anniversary of her canonisation, Sister Mary Prema Pierick, Superior-General of the Missionaries of Charity, announced that Mother Teresa would be made the co-patron of the Calcutta Archdiocese during a Mass in the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Rosary_Cathedral,_Kolkata" title="Holy Rosary Cathedral, Kolkata">Cathedral of the Most Holy Rosary</a> on 6 September 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>  On 5 September 2017, Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Thomas_D%27Souza" title="Thomas D&#39;Souza">Thomas D'Souza</a>, who serves as head of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Calcutta" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Calcutta">Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Calcutta</a>, confirmed that Mother Teresa would be named co-patron of the Calcutta Diocese, alongside Francis Xavier.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> On 6 September 2017, about 500 people attended the Mass at a cathedral where Dominique Gomes, the local Vicar General,<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> read the decree instituting her as the second patron saint of the archdiocese.<sup id="cite_ref-mysacanpl8-1_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mysacanpl8-1-166">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> The ceremony was also presided over by D'Souza and the Vatican's ambassador to India, <a href="/wiki/Giambattista_Diquattro" title="Giambattista Diquattro">Giambattista Diquattro</a>, who lead the Mass and inaugurated a bronze statue in the church of Mother Teresa carrying a child.<sup id="cite_ref-mysacanpl8-1_166-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mysacanpl8-1-166">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Catholic Church declared St. Francis Xavier the first patron saint of Calcutta in 1986.<sup id="cite_ref-mysacanpl8-1_166-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mysacanpl8-1-166">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy_and_depictions_in_popular_culture">Legacy and depictions in popular culture</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Commemorations">Commemorations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Commemorations_of_Mother_Teresa" title="Commemorations of Mother Teresa">Commemorations of Mother Teresa</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Terminal_jashte.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Airport terminal, with four trees in the foreground" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Terminal_jashte.jpg/220px-Terminal_jashte.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Terminal_jashte.jpg/330px-Terminal_jashte.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Terminal_jashte.jpg/440px-Terminal_jashte.jpg 2x" data-file-width="583" data-file-height="389" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Terminal_jashte.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Tirana_International_Airport_N%C3%ABn%C3%AB_Tereza" title="Tirana International Airport Nënë Tereza">Tirana International Airport Nënë Tereza</a></div></div></div>
<p>Mother Teresa has been commemorated by museums and named the patroness of a number of churches. She has had buildings, roads and complexes named after her, including <a href="/wiki/Tirana_International_Airport_N%C3%ABn%C3%AB_Tereza" title="Tirana International Airport Nënë Tereza">Albania's international airport</a>. <a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Mother Teresa Day">Mother Teresa Day</a> (<span title="Albanian-language text"><i lang="sq">Dita e Nënë Terezës</i></span>), 19 October, is a <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Albania" title="Public holidays in Albania">public holiday in Albania</a>. In 2009, the <a href="/wiki/Memorial_House_of_Mother_Teresa" title="Memorial House of Mother Teresa">Memorial House of Mother Teresa</a> was opened in her hometown of Skopje, <a href="/wiki/North_Macedonia" title="North Macedonia">North Macedonia</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_Blessed_Mother_Teresa_in_Pristina" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathedral of Blessed Mother Teresa in Pristina">Cathedral of Blessed Mother Teresa</a> in <a href="/wiki/Pristina" title="Pristina">Pristina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>, is named in her honour.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> The demolition of a historic high school building to make way for the new construction initially sparked controversy in the local community, but the high school was later relocated to a new, more spacious campus. Consecrated on 5 September 2017, it became the first cathedral in Mother Teresa's honour and the second extant one in Kosovo.<sup id="cite_ref-jfjpqpvapv_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jfjpqpvapv-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:152px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg/150px-Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="128" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg/225px-Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg/300px-Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3353" data-file-height="2865" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Mother_Teresa_Cathedral_Prishtina8.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_Saint_Mother_Teresa,_Pristina" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathedral of Saint Mother Teresa, Pristina">Cathedral of Saint Mother Teresa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prishtin%C3%AB" class="mw-redirect" title="Prishtinë">Prishtinë</a></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa_Women%27s_University" title="Mother Teresa Women&#39;s University">Mother Teresa Women's University</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> in <a href="/wiki/Kodaikanal" title="Kodaikanal">Kodaikanal</a>, was established in 1984 as a public university by the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Tamil_Nadu" title="Government of Tamil Nadu">government of Tamil Nadu</a>. The Mother Teresa Postgraduate and Research Institute of Health Sciences,<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> in <a href="/wiki/Pondicherry_(city)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pondicherry (city)">Pondicherry</a>, was established in 1999 by the government of <a href="/wiki/Puducherry_(union_territory)" title="Puducherry (union territory)">Puducherry</a>. The charitable organisation <a href="/wiki/Sevalaya" title="Sevalaya">Sevalaya</a> runs the <a href="/wiki/Sevalaya#Mother_Teresa_Girls_Home" title="Sevalaya">Mother Teresa Girls Home</a>, providing poor and orphaned girls near the underserved village of Kasuva in Tamil Nadu with free food, clothing, shelter and education.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> A number of tributes by Mother Teresa's biographer, Navin Chawla, have appeared in Indian newspapers and magazines.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Indian_Railways" title="Indian Railways">Indian Railways</a> introduced the "Mother Express", a new train named after Mother Teresa, on 26 August 2010 to commemorate the centenary of her birth.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a> government organised centenary celebrations honouring Mother Teresa on 4 December 2010 in <a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Chennai</a>, headed by chief minister <a href="/wiki/M_Karunanidhi" class="mw-redirect" title="M Karunanidhi">M Karunanidhi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> Beginning on 5 September 2013, the anniversary of her death has been designated the <a href="/wiki/International_Day_of_Charity" title="International Day of Charity">International Day of Charity</a> by the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2012, Mother Teresa was ranked number 5 in <a href="/wiki/Outlook_(Indian_magazine)" title="Outlook (Indian magazine)">Outlook India's</a> poll of <a href="/wiki/The_Greatest_Indian" title="The Greatest Indian">the Greatest Indian</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Sacred_Heart_University" title="Sacred Heart University">Sacred Heart University</a> in <a href="/wiki/Fairfield,_Connecticut" title="Fairfield, Connecticut">Fairfield, Connecticut</a>, has a residence hall named after her, called Teresa of Calcutta Hall.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Film_and_literature">Film and literature</span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Documentaries_and_books">Documentaries and books</span></h4>
<ul><li>Mother Teresa is the subject of the 1969 documentary film and 1972 book, <i><a href="/wiki/Something_Beautiful_for_God" title="Something Beautiful for God">Something Beautiful for God</a></i>, by <a href="/wiki/Malcolm_Muggeridge" title="Malcolm Muggeridge">Malcolm Muggeridge</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> The film has been credited with drawing the Western world's attention to Mother Teresa.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Christopher_Hitchens" title="Christopher Hitchens">Christopher Hitchens</a>' 1994 documentary, <i><a href="/wiki/Hell%27s_Angel_(documentary)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hell&#39;s Angel (documentary)">Hell's Angel</a></i>, argues that Mother Teresa urged the poor to accept their fate; the rich are portrayed as favoured by God.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> It was the precursor of Hitchens' essay, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Missionary_Position:_Mother_Teresa_in_Theory_and_Practice" title="The Missionary Position: Mother Teresa in Theory and Practice">The Missionary Position: Mother Teresa in Theory and Practice</a></i>.</li>
<li><i>Mother of The Century</i> (2001) and <i>Mother Teresa</i> (2002) are <a href="/wiki/Short_film" title="Short film">short</a> <a href="/wiki/Documentary_film" title="Documentary film">documentary films</a>, about the life and work of Mother Teresa among the poor of India, directed by Amar Kumar Bhattacharya. They were produced by the <a href="/wiki/Films_Division_of_India" title="Films Division of India">Films Division</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_India" title="Government of India">Government of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa:_No_Greater_Love" title="Mother Teresa: No Greater Love">Mother Teresa: No Greater Love</a></i> (2022) is a documentary film featuring unusual access to institutional archives and how her vision to serve Christ among the poor is being implemented through the Missionaries of Charity.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dramatic_films_and_television">Dramatic films and television</span></h4>
<ul><li>Mother Teresa appeared in <i><a href="/wiki/Bible_Ki_Kahaniyan" title="Bible Ki Kahaniyan">Bible Ki Kahaniyan</a></i>, an Indian Christian show based on the Bible which aired on <a href="/wiki/DD_National" title="DD National">DD National</a> during the early 1990s. She introduced some of the episodes, laying down the importance of the Bible's message.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Geraldine_Chaplin" title="Geraldine Chaplin">Geraldine Chaplin</a> played Mother Teresa in <i><a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa:_In_the_Name_of_God%27s_Poor" title="Mother Teresa: In the Name of God&#39;s Poor">Mother Teresa: In the Name of God's Poor</a></i>, which received a 1997 Art Film Festival award.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>She was played by <a href="/wiki/Olivia_Hussey" title="Olivia Hussey">Olivia Hussey</a> in a 2003 Italian television miniseries, <i><a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa_of_Calcutta_(film)" title="Mother Teresa of Calcutta (film)">Mother Teresa of Calcutta</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Re-released in 2007, it received a <a href="/wiki/CAMIE_Awards" title="CAMIE Awards">CAMIE award</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Mother Teresa was played by <a href="/wiki/Juliet_Stevenson" title="Juliet Stevenson">Juliet Stevenson</a> in the 2014 film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Letters_(2014_film)" title="The Letters (2014 film)">The Letters</a></i>, which was based on her letters to Vatican priest <a href="/wiki/Celeste_van_Exem" title="Celeste van Exem">Celeste van Exem</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Mother Teresa, played by Cara Francis the FantasyGrandma, rap battled <a href="/wiki/Sigmund_Freud" title="Sigmund Freud">Sigmund Freud</a> in <a href="/wiki/Epic_Rap_Battles_of_History" title="Epic Rap Battles of History">Epic Rap Battles of History</a>, a comedy rap <a href="/wiki/YouTube" title="YouTube">YouTube</a> series created by <a href="/wiki/Nice_Peter" title="Nice Peter">Nice Peter</a> and <a href="/wiki/Epic_Lloyd" class="mw-redirect" title="Epic Lloyd">Epic Lloyd</a>. The rap was released on YouTube 22 September 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In the 2020 animated film <i><a href="/wiki/Soul_(2020_film)" title="Soul (2020 film)">Soul</a></i>, Mother Teresa briefly appears as one of 22's past mentors.</li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-6"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-6">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">After World War I and the dissolution of the Ottoman Empire, Skopje became part of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbs,_Croats_and_Slovenes" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Serbs, Croats and Slovenes">Kingdom of Serbs, Croats and Slovenes</a>, for the duration of Teresa's childhood. Since the 1990s, Skopje has been the capital of <a href="/wiki/North_Macedonia" title="North Macedonia">North Macedonia</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-7"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-7">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Albanian: <i lang="sq">Shën Tereza e Kalkutës</i>; Bengali: <span lang="bn">কলকাতার সন্ত টেরিজা</span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-17">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Although some sources state she was 10 when her father died, in an interview with her brother, the Vatican documents her age at the time as "about eight".</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<div class="navbox-styles"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1061467846"/></div><div role="navigation" class="navbox" aria-labelledby="Recipients_of_Bharat_Ratna_Award" style="padding:3px"><table class="nowraplinks mw-collapsible autocollapse navbox-inner" style="border-spacing:0;background:transparent;color:inherit"><tbody><tr><th scope="col" class="navbox-title" colspan="2" style="background: #BFBFBF"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1063604349"/><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Bharat_Ratna" title="Template:Bharat Ratna"><abbr title="View this template" style="background: #BFBFBF;;background:none transparent;border:none;box-shadow:none;padding:0;">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Bharat_Ratna" title="Template talk:Bharat Ratna"><abbr title="Discuss this template" style="background: #BFBFBF;;background:none transparent;border:none;box-shadow:none;padding:0;">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Bharat_Ratna&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template" style="background: #BFBFBF;;background:none transparent;border:none;box-shadow:none;padding:0;">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div><div id="Recipients_of_Bharat_Ratna_Award" style="font-size:114%;margin:0 4em">Recipients of <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Ratna" title="Bharat Ratna">Bharat Ratna</a> Award</div></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="navbox-group" style="background: #BFBFBF;width:1%">1954–1960</th><td class="navbox-list-with-group navbox-list navbox-odd hlist" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/C._Rajagopalachari" title="C. Rajagopalachari">C. Rajagopalachari</a>, <a href="/wiki/S._Radhakrishnan" class="mw-redirect" title="S. Radhakrishnan">S. Radhakrishnan</a>, and <a href="/wiki/C._V._Raman" title="C. V. Raman">C. V. Raman</a> (1954)</li>
